# M7 W2 M4 — POST-HOC read-outs over the twelve NEUTRALITY traces

Generated by `wrappers/readouts_driver_m4.py`; **no solver ran here.**
Every figure is machine-written from this task's own flat traces, whose
sha256 are in `traces-m4/SHA256-parent-traces-m4.txt`, and from the slices in
`traces-m4/`. The tail finder is `readouts_m4.py`'s, unedited.

**POST-HOC.** RULE.md §6 was pre-registered for §1.1's TEN cells and for
phase-3 legs that §8 stopped before they ran. It was never pre-registered
for §1.2's three neutrality cells. Nothing here is a verdict of the rule,
and RULE.md is not edited.

## §6.2's predicate, per solve, per cell × arm

`N` passes; `p` the winning period (`-` = no exactly-periodic suffix at
any p ≤ 64); `|T|` the tail. `REC` = RECURRENT_TAIL (T1∧T2∧T3),
`GONE` = ¬REC ∧ Σ‖Δx‖∞>0.

**No minor-cap column is printed.** The flat trace carries ONE `max_iter`
in its header, and that value is not the cap every QP in the run used — the
four arms' own headers on these three cells read 770/770/770/1770,
1170×4 and 1380/1380/2340/2760 — so a per-solve `capped` column derived
from it would be wrong for every solve but the captured one. Solve LENGTH
is printed instead and nothing is inferred from it.

## Totals — solves, RECURRENT_TAIL, RECURRENCE_GONE

`recurrent minors` is the sum of N over that arm's RECURRENT solves;
`all minors` the sum over every solve, which equals the row's own
`qp_minor_iters`.

| cell | arm | solves | RECURRENT | GONE | recurrent minors | all minors | recurrent share |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| READING6 | off | 41 | 3 | 38 | 2310 | 3833 | 0.603 |
| READING6 | E | 41 | 2 | 39 | 1540 | 3119 | 0.494 |
| READING6 | L | 41 | 3 | 38 | 2310 | 3833 | 0.603 |
| READING6 | L+ | 7 | 5 | 2 | 8600 | 8761 | 0.982 |
| SMBANK | off | 29 | 0 | 29 | 0 | 334 | 0.000 |
| SMBANK | E | 29 | 0 | 29 | 0 | 328 | 0.000 |
| SMBANK | L | 29 | 0 | 29 | 0 | 334 | 0.000 |
| SMBANK | L+ | 29 | 0 | 29 | 0 | 334 | 0.000 |
| ZAMB2-8 | off | 35 | 3 | 32 | 4620 | 6666 | 0.693 |
| ZAMB2-8 | E | 33 | 4 | 29 | 7920 | 9947 | 0.796 |
| ZAMB2-8 | L | 28 | 2 | 26 | 3240 | 5016 | 0.646 |
| ZAMB2-8 | L+ | 16 | 4 | 12 | 8340 | 10356 | 0.805 |

### The two questions the close-out asks, answered from the rows above

* **READING6.** OFF has 3 RECURRENT solves carrying 2310 of its 3833 minors (60.3%); E has 2 carrying 1540 of its 3119 (49.4%). Minors move 3833 → 3119 (-714); minors inside RECURRENT solves move 2310 → 1540 (-770); minors outside them move 1523 → 1579 (+56).
* **ZAMB2-8.** OFF has 3 RECURRENT solves carrying 4620 of its 6666 minors (69.3%); E has 4 carrying 7920 of its 9947 (79.6%). Minors move 6666 → 9947 (+3281); minors inside RECURRENT solves move 4620 → 7920 (+3300); minors outside them move 2046 → 2027 (-19).

**Read with care (POST-HOC):** a solve INDEX is comparable across two arms only up to the first solve at which the two walks diverge — measured here as the lowest index whose record BLOCK is not byte-identical between the two arms:

* `READING6` off vs E: first diverging solve index 0 (off 46 records, E 46)
* `READING6` off vs L: first diverging solve index 19 (off 313 records, L 313)
* `READING6` off vs L+: first diverging solve index 0 (off 46 records, L+ 46)
* `SMBANK` off vs E: first diverging solve index 0 (off 32 records, E 34)
* `SMBANK` off vs L: no diverging solve — every record block is byte-identical
* `SMBANK` off vs L+: first diverging solve index 1 (off 28 records, L+ 28)
* `ZAMB2-8` off vs E: first diverging solve index 0 (off 1380 records, E 1380)
* `ZAMB2-8` off vs L: first diverging solve index 8 (off 427 records, L 427)
* `ZAMB2-8` off vs L+: first diverging solve index 0 (off 1380 records, L+ 1380)

Past that point the same index is not the same subproblem, so only the AGGREGATE columns above are read, never a per-index pairing.

## The walk difference the ROW gate cannot see

The neutrality gate of RULE.md §5.5 compares two ROWS. A row carries totals. Two walks can take different routes and arrive at identical totals, and then the gate reports them identical. The table below compares the two runs' PER-MINOR RECORDS instead, index by index, byte for byte, over the solves both runs have. It is POST-HOC and it decides nothing — but it is the only place the difference between "inert" and "invisible at the row" is visible.

| cell | arm | solves off / arm | solves with ≥ 1 differing record | differing records | first differing record |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| READING6 | E | 41 / 41 | 15 | 3393 | solve 0, minor 6, first fields `alpha`, `dx_inf`, `raw_ratio` |
| READING6 | L | 41 / 41 | 1 | 60 | solve 19, minor 180, first fields `block_index`, `decision_multiplier`, `ws_sig_out` |
| READING6 | L+ | 41 / 7 | 7 | 8630 | solve 0, minor 41, first fields `block_index`, `decision_multiplier`, `ws_sig_out` |
| SMBANK | E | 29 / 29 | 29 | 332 | solve 0, minor 6, first fields `alpha`, `dx_inf`, `raw_ratio` |
| SMBANK | L | 29 / 29 | 0 | 0 | — (every shared record block is byte-identical) |
| SMBANK | L+ | 29 / 29 | 3 | 20 | solve 1, minor 15, first fields `block_index`, `decision_multiplier`, `ws_sig_out` |
| ZAMB2-8 | E | 35 / 33 | 23 | 11263 | solve 0, minor 0, first fields `block_index`, `alpha`, `dx_inf`, `raw_ratio`, `ws_sig_out` |
| ZAMB2-8 | L | 35 / 28 | 20 | 2597 | solve 8, minor 402, first fields `block_index`, `bound_gap`, `decision_multiplier`, `ws_sig_out` |
| ZAMB2-8 | L+ | 35 / 16 | 16 | 9974 | solve 0, minor 98, first fields `block_index`, `bound_gap`, `decision_multiplier`, `ws_sig_out` |

The solve named in the last column is SLICED into `traces-m4/` under every arm that has it, alongside the RECURRENT ones, so the first place two walks part can be read record by record.

## §6.6's per-solve read-outs on the RECURRENT solves

The union of RECURRENT solve indices per cell is taken over all four
arms, and each such index is read under EVERY arm that has it, so a
recurrence one arm carries and another does not is visible from both
sides. An index that does not exist under an arm is reported ABSENT and
never silently dropped. The divergence caveat above applies to every
cross-arm reading of a single index.

### READING6 — RECURRENT indices (union over arms) plus every first-divergence index above: 0, 1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, 19, 35
